> DEVELOPER

Is it confusing where they are? Do they use both the language specific and overall ones or do the language specific ones override the other?

> AGENT

Short answer: it’s a little split, and yes they use both.

- Shared “subjective questions” (actual scoring/review prompts) live in:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/data/per_file_dimensions.json`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/data/holistic_dimensions.json`
- Language-specific guidance (extra hints like TS/Python/C# patterns/auth/naming) lives in:
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/review.py`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/review.py`
  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/csharp/review.py`

How precedence works:

- `lang_guidance` does **not** override `dimension_prompts`; it’s additive context.
- Both are included in the prepared payload (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py:68` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py:161`) and written to `.desloppify/query.json`.
- One exception: for holistic mode, each language can override **which dimensions are selected** via `holistic_review_dimensions` in each language `__init__.py`, but prompt text still comes from the shared holistic JSON.

> DEVELOPER

So when would they override them?

> AGENT

They don’t really override each other today, except one specific case.

- `lang_guidance` (`patterns/auth/naming`) is additive context only, not a replacement for prompts:  
  `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py:73` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py:168`

- Prompt text (`dimension_prompts` + `system_prompt`) always comes from shared JSON files:  
  `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/data/per_file_dimensions.json`  
  `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/data/holistic_dimensions.json`

Override order is:

1. Explicit CLI `--dimensions` wins (both per-file and holistic):  
   `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py:65` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py:157`
2. Holistic only: if no CLI `--dimensions`, language `holistic_review_dimensions` is used before global holistic defaults:  
   `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py:157`
3. Per-file: if no CLI `--dimensions`, it uses config `review_dimensions`, then global `DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS`; no per-language override there:  
   `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py:65`

So language-specific content can override the **dimension set** in holistic mode, but not the shared prompt definitions themselves.

> DEVELOPER

IS this beautifully structued?

> AGENT

Not yet. It’s solid, but not “beautiful.”

- Good: concerns are separated cleanly between shared prompt data (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/data/per_file_dimensions.json`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/data/holistic_dimensions.json`) and language plugins (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/*/review.py`).
- Confusing: behavior is spread across multiple places (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/dimensions_lang.py`, language `__init__.py`), so precedence is not obvious from one file.
- Design smell: language configs can override holistic dimension selection, but cannot override per-dimension prompt text. That asymmetry is easy to misunderstand.

If you want it “beautiful,” I’d make precedence explicit in one function/module and document it as code, not tribal knowledge.

I reviewed the prompts in:

- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/_shared/review_data/per_file_dimensions.json`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/_shared/review_data/holistic_dimensions.json`

I interpreted your “five” as the beauty-adjacent set with highest overlap risk: `high_level_elegance`, `mid_level_elegance`, `low_level_elegance`, `abstraction_fitness`, `cross_module_architecture`.

**Critical Assessment**
1. The high/mid/low split is directionally good, but wording is still a bit poetic (`boundary dignity`, `conceptual compression`) and not always falsifiable.
2. `high_level_elegance` overlaps with `cross_module_architecture` and `package_organization`.
3. `mid_level_elegance` overlaps with `api_surface_coherence` and parts of `error_consistency`.
4. `low_level_elegance` overlaps with `logic_clarity`, `type_safety`, and `error_consistency` unless tightly gated.
5. Overlap control is currently prompt-only (`skip` text), not mechanically enforced, so interlop can still happen in practice.

**Tighter, More Succinct Five Questions**
1. `high_level_elegance`: “Is the structural decomposition clear, domain-aligned, and easy to explain?”
2. `mid_level_elegance`: “Are module handoffs explicit, minimal, and predictable?”
3. `low_level_elegance`: “Are function/class internals direct, local, and proportionate?”
4. `abstraction_fitness`: “Do abstractions pay rent, or are they speculative ceremony?”
5. `cross_module_architecture`: “Is dependency direction intentional, acyclic, and bounded in blast radius?”

**How To Prevent Interlop**
1. Use scope-of-evidence routing:
   - Codebase/package evidence -> `high_level_elegance` or `cross_module_architecture`
   - Inter-file/interface evidence -> `mid_level_elegance`
   - Intra-function/class evidence -> `low_level_elegance`
2. Use tie-break precedence:
   - Hard-violation dimensions win first (`cross_module_architecture`, `abstraction_fitness`, `type_safety`, `dependency_health`, `error_consistency`)
   - Elegance dimensions only capture residual quality not already covered.
3. Enforce “one evidence unit, one owner”:
   - Same concrete symptom should not produce multiple findings across these five.

If you want, I can now rewrite those five prompt blocks in both JSON files to this stricter form.

And are the questons detailed enough? Are they self-explanatory? Think thisnthrough

> AGENT

Short answer: **not fully yet**.

They’re strong conceptually, but still a bit **under-specified operationally** in  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/_shared/review_data/per_file_dimensions.json` and  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/_shared/review_data/holistic_dimensions.json`.

1. **Detailed enough?**  
Partly. `look_for` is detailed, but mostly descriptive rather than decision-grade. It tells reviewers what to notice, not always when to file or skip.

2. **Self-explanatory?**  
Partly. `abstraction_fitness` and `cross_module_architecture` are fairly self-explanatory. The 3 elegance dimensions are less so because terms like “elegance” can mean different things without strict scope.

3. **Main gaps causing overlap**  
1. No explicit “unit of analysis” rule (function vs file vs module vs codebase).  
2. No tie-break rule when two dimensions fit the same symptom.  
3. No minimum evidence rule (single incident vs recurring pattern).  
4. High-level elegance still overlaps with architecture/package dimensions; low-level overlaps with logic/error/type dimensions.

4. **What would make them truly self-explanatory**  
1. Add one explicit ownership sentence per dimension: “This dimension owns X.”  
2. Add one exclusion sentence per dimension: “If Y, use Z dimension instead.”  
3. Add evidence threshold: “Only file if seen in N places” (for holistic) or “must affect behavior/maintainability” (for per-file).  
4. Add tie-break precedence: hard-violation dimensions win; elegance gets residual quality signal.

No, not fully obvious for a first-time user.

1. Is it obvious what to do?
- Partly.
- `next` shows “Subjective Focus” and tells you to run `desloppify review --prepare`, which helps.
- But it’s still confusing that:
  - `subjective_review` findings are mostly review-coverage debt (changed/unreviewed files),
  - while elegance scores come from imported assessments,
  - and `review` findings can be zero at the same time.

2. Is it obvious how to increase those scores?
- Not enough.
- A newcomer may not realize there are two different levers:
  - reduce `subjective_review` coverage findings by reviewing files,
  - improve elegance dimensions via new review assessments (ideally after real code changes).
- It can feel non-transparent because scores can change after assessment import even without direct detector findings.

3. Are they tracked nicely?
- Technically yes, UX-wise mixed.
- Data is tracked cleanly in state (`dimension_scores`, `subjective_assessments`), but the mental model is split across multiple concepts and commands.

If you want, I can implement a clearer UX pass so `next/scan` explicitly says:
- “Coverage debt” vs “Quality assessment,”

> AGENT

It’s **much better and holistically structured**, but I’d say it’s **not fully “beautiful” yet**.

What’s strong now:
- Clear two-lane mental model: `quality score` vs `coverage debt`.
- Concrete commands are attached to each lane.
- Guidance appears consistently in both `scan` and `next`.

What still feels un-beautiful:
- Slight verbosity/repetition across outputs.
- Too many equally-weighted prompts at once (not one dominant “do this now”).
- No explicit “done state” per lane (what target finishes this loop).

If you want, I can do one more polish pass: make it a tighter 3-step flow (`Now`, `Then`, `Done when...`) with explicit targets.

> DEVELOPER

Think this throgh frmo an agent perspective and make a plan

> AGENT

From an agent perspective, the goal is: zero ambiguity about “what to do next” and “how to know I’m done.”

**Plan**
1. Define a single subjective decision engine (`primary lane` selector).
2. Encode lane priority rules:
3. `coverage debt` (open `subjective_review`) first if high.
4. `unassessed dimensions` (0% placeholders) next.
5. `low assessed dimensions` (< target, e.g. 95) next.
6. `open review findings` cleanup lane in parallel.
7. Make `scan` and `next` consume the same guidance object so messaging is identical.
8. Render guidance in strict structure:
9. `Now:` one command only.
10. `Why:` one sentence tied to current state numbers.
11. `Done when:` explicit condition (numeric threshold).
12. `Then:` next lane command.
13. Add state-backed tracking (`subjective_guidance`) so agents can see current lane, target, and progress over scans.
14. Tighten wording to deterministic/actionable language; remove vague prose.
15. Add tests for lane selection and output consistency across:
16. `scan` output,
17. `next` output,
18. scoped `next --scope subjective_review`.
19. Add one acceptance test with a realistic mixed state (coverage debt + low elegance scores) verifying the chosen primary lane and command.
20. Ship and validate by running full focused suite + real `scan`/`next` smoke checks.

**Success Criteria**
1. On every `scan` and `next`, there is exactly one “Now” command.
2. Agents can tell in <10 seconds what action most improves subjective health.
3. No contradiction between `scan` guidance and `next` guidance.
4. Clear completion conditions for each lane.

> AGENT

Yes. `scan` is the heartbeat, and `next` is the dispatcher.

`scan` is where state is recomputed and guidance is generated (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan.py:71`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_summary.py:32`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_analysis.py:50`).  
`next` is where the system tells you the exact next fix/resolve action (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:374`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:292`).

**User Journeys**
1. Baseline / first-time run  
Run `desloppify scan --path .`, then `desloppify status`, then `desloppify next` (also documented in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/README.md:20`).  
If no state exists, commands explicitly say to run scan (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/status.py:43`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/plan_cmd.py:15`).  
Success signal: score block + detector progress + first “Suggested next”.

2. Mechanical cleanup loop (core daily loop)  
Flow: `scan` -> `fix --dry-run` or `next` -> `resolve` -> `scan`.  
`next` shows tier navigator, one item, and exact command to run (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:81`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:307`).  
`resolve` enforces note/attestation and then shows score deltas; it reminds that verified score is confirmed after scan (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/resolve.py:52`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/resolve.py:129`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/resolve.py:138`).

3. Auto-fixer fast lane  
Start from narrative “run fixer first” recommendations and `next` autofix hints (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative/actions.py:79`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:198`).  
`fix` prints dry-run samples and asks review questions before apply (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py:232`).  
After apply, it explicitly says rescan (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py:214`).

4. Subjective quality loop (coverage + quality)  
`scan` prints a dedicated “Subjective path” with separate commands for quality deficit vs coverage debt (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_dimensions.py:174`).  
`review --prepare` writes review payload and tells you to import findings (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/review_cmd.py:90`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/review_cmd.py:95`).  
`review --import` reports what changed and points to `issues` if needed (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/review_cmd.py:144`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/review_cmd.py:175`).

5. Investigation queue journey (for review findings)  
`issues` lists open review findings by impact, flags uninvestigated, and gives next command (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/issues_cmd.py:32`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/issues_cmd.py:59`).  
`issues show N` for detail, `issues update N --file ...` to attach investigation, then resolve (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/issues_cmd.py:72`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/issues_cmd.py:149`).

6. Target-score (north-star) journey  
Set target: `desloppify config set target_strict_score <N>` (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/config.py:26`).  
Both `scan` and `next` now show strict-vs-target gap and target-based subjective threshold (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan.py:98`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_dimensions.py:214`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:332`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:346`).

7. Planning / parallelization journey  
`scan` narrative can suggest fixers-first and parallel lanes (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_analysis.py:50`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative/strategy.py:239`).  
`plan` gives a markdown backlog by tier/file and includes subjective action items (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/plan_render.py:125`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/plan_render.py:159`).

**How users know what to do next**
1. `scan` gives strategy/top action and dimension-level hints (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_analysis.py:57`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_dimensions.py:100`).  
2. `next` gives exact per-item command, with tier fallback/explain (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:253`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:400`).  
3. `status` gives focus dimension and review summary (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/status.py:260`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/status.py:357`).  
4. Every command also writes structured handoff data to `.desloppify/query.json` (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/_helpers.py:32`).

**Bottom line**
- Yes: `scan` is the heartbeat.  
- `next` is the immediate execution queue.  
- `status` is the compass.  
- `review/issues` are the subjective track.  
- `target_strict_score` is the north star.

> AGENT

Ranking is deterministic and currently biased toward mechanical work first.

1. `next` ranking pipeline  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:352` builds queue items from findings, then sorts at `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:385`.

2. Why subjective/elegance is not higher  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:186` forces all review/subjective findings to `effective_tier=4`.  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:203` sorts by:
- mechanical: tier asc, confidence asc, count desc, id
- subjective: tier asc, then explicitly after mechanical (`1` flag), then subjective score asc  
So any open T1/T2/T3 will outrank elegance items by policy.

3. Extra reason it feels buried  
Synthetic subjective items include all subjective dimensions with score `<100`, not `<target>`, at `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:125`.  
That adds low-priority subjective noise even when dimension is above target.

4. Issues queue ranking (separate from `next`)  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/issues.py:46` sorts open review findings by computed weight (confidence + holistic multiplier), so `desloppify issues` is the right place for elegance work ordering.

Messaging clarity:
- Mostly understandable, but not fully obvious.
- Good: `next` footer explicitly says open review findings and points to `desloppify issues` (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:357`).
- Not great:
1. `desloppify issues -h` does not advertise default “list” behavior (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py:285`).
2. Subjective item action can point to `review --prepare` even when review findings are already open because dimension keys are mismatched (`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:116` vs `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:129`).

If you want, I can patch this so elegance items are clearly surfaced when they are the strict-score bottleneck.

- Per-file (10): `naming_quality`, `error_consistency`, `abstraction_fitness`, `logic_clarity`, `ai_generated_debt`, `type_safety`, `contract_coherence`, `high_level_elegance`, `mid_level_elegance`, `low_level_elegance`
- Holistic (Python-curated 11): `cross_module_architecture`, `convention_outlier`, `error_consistency`, `abstraction_fitness`, `dependency_health`, `test_strategy`, `ai_generated_debt`, `package_organization`, `high_level_elegance`, `mid_level_elegance`, `low_level_elegance`

  - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/review_data/holistic_dimensions.override.json`

**How ranking/scoring works toward them**
- Subjective scores come from imported `assessments`; findings are work items, not direct score penalties.
- Holistic assessments override per-file for the same dimension.
- Queue ranking: subjective items are forced to Tier 4 and sorted after mechanical items; lowest subjective scores are surfaced as below-target work.

Sources:
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/import_findings.py:17`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:129`

- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:338`

**Are they evenly weighted?**
- Not across the whole score.
- Overall blend is fixed: subjective = 25%, mechanical = 75%.
- Within the subjective pool, active subjective dimensions are effectively equal-weight (all Tier 4; no sample dampening there).

Sources:
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py:149`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py:468`
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py:488`

**Is beauty/elegance score communicated?**
- Yes in CLI outputs (`status`, `next`, scan follow-up), as separate `High/Mid/Low Elegance` percentages.
- There is no single combined “beauty score” field; it’s three dimensions plus overall strict/objective scores.
- Current stored values (from `state-python.json`, dated 2026-02-17): High `76`, Mid `70`, Low `72`.

> AGENT

Yes. From my perspective, this was mostly a **tooling-priority failure**, not just an agent diligence failure.

Why it happened:

1. Subjective work is intentionally de-prioritized in ranking.
`subjective` items are forced to Tier 4 and sorted after mechanical items, so an agent following `next` naturally keeps doing mechanical work first.  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:129`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:232`

2. A 0% elegance score can be a “missing assessment placeholder,” not an open finding.
When no assessment exists, score is set to `0.0` by default for subjective dimensions. That looks severe but is not treated as a hard blocker in workflow.  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py:414`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py:449`

3. The recommended recovery command is ambiguous for this case.
The scan/next guidance says `desloppify review --prepare --dimensions ...` (non-holistic), which can return “0 files” and falsely imply coverage is complete when holistic reassessment is what’s needed.  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_dimensions.py:231`  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:338`

4. `fix review` can produce a false “done” signal.
If per-file candidates are zero, it prints “Nothing to do” and exits early, without forcing a holistic refresh path.  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py:71`

5. Scan post-analysis only hard-nudges `review` findings, not `subjective_review` coverage debt.
So if there are no open `review` findings, the stronger reminder may not fire even when subjective state is stale/unassessed.  
`/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_analysis.py:67`

So your diagnosis is right: the agent optimized for deterministic closure, and the product’s current priority semantics allowed that.

If you want, I can implement guardrails so this can’t happen again:
1. Treat any subjective dimension at 0% as blocking in `next`.
2. Auto-suggest `desloppify review --prepare --holistic --refresh` (not plain `review --prepare`) for 0%/stale elegance.
3. Make `fix review` fall through to holistic guidance instead of “Nothing to do” when subjective dims are below target.
4. Add a scan-time “cannot declare complete” warning when subjective dims are unassessed or below target.

Implemented a balanced approach: subjective integrity is now high-visibility, but not hard-blocking.

What changed:

1. `scan` now explicitly flags integrity gaps (unassessed 0% placeholders and holistic stale/missing) with a near-top action:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_dimensions.py:158`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_dimensions.py:241`
   - It now points to `desloppify review --prepare --holistic --refresh`, then import + rescan.

2. `next` now separates:
   - quality tuning (low assessed subjective dimensions), vs
   - integrity gaps (unassessed + holistic stale/missing),
   and prioritizes the integrity command when needed:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:55`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py:326`

3. `fix review` no longer gives a false “nothing to do” signal when per-file candidates are zero but subjective integrity is still stale/unassessed:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py:71`
   - It now prints holistic refresh guidance in that case.

4. Queue action commands are clearer:
   - subjective coverage findings now default to triage/holistic commands instead of generic resolve commands.
   - unassessed subjective dimension items now default to holistic refresh.
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:95`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py:165`

5. `scan` post-analysis now also surfaces subjective integrity signals directly:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_analysis.py:67`

This keeps balance:
- Mechanical T1/T2/T3 priority is unchanged.
- Only integrity-risk subjective states get elevated messaging.

Validation:
- `pytest -q tests/test_work_queue.py tests/test_cmd_next.py tests/test_cmd_fix_review.py tests/test_scan_reporting_direct.py` → `44 passed`
- `pytest -q tests/test_cmd_scan.py tests/test_plan.py` → `82 passed`

If you want, next step is to tune wording severity (e.g. “High-priority” vs softer phrasing) without changing behavior.

> DEVELOPER

And is this all very well structured? For example, is it easy for you to understand what the overall flow looks like and where the gap was here?

> AGENT

Mostly yes. The flow is understandable now, and the gap is clear.

Current flow is coherent end-to-end:
1. Coverage/staleness is detected in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/base.py` via `phase_subjective_review`.
2. Subjective scores (including 0% placeholders) are materialized in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py`.
3. Priority/action routing is decided in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/work_queue.py`.
4. User guidance is rendered in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_dimensions.py`, `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/next.py`, and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/fix_cmd.py`.

Where the gap was:
- 0% subjective placeholders looked like generic “low quality” rather than “integrity missing”.
- `subjective_review` items were always T4 and easy to overlook.
- Some messaging pointed to per-dimension review, not holistic refresh.
- `fix review` could say “Nothing to do” even when holistic/unassessed debt still existed.

Remaining structural caveat:
- Holistic-gap detection logic is duplicated in multiple command files (string/id heuristics). It works, but centralizing that logic into one helper would make it cleaner and safer against drift.

# Elegance-95 Full Improvement Plan (No Score Gaming)

## Summary
1. Baseline (scan on February 17, 2026): `259` open findings, strict `95.0`, elegance scores `High 84 / Mid 80 / Low 82`.
2. Goal: improve every currently-open detector bucket in parallel, raise `High/Mid/Low Elegance` to `>=95`, and keep strict `>=95`.
3. Delivery style locked from your preferences: all scopes in parallel, large sweeps, one-shot path migration, tactical suppressions allowed only with explicit justification.

## Anti-Gaming Contract (Hard Rules)
1. Every sweep must include substantive code deltas before any subjective refresh/import.
2. Subjective refresh runs every sweep, but only with a written “code delta -> expected elegance impact” rationale in the sweep log.
3. Tactical suppressions/wontfix are allowed in any zone, but capped at `<=8` total for the whole campaign.
4. Every suppression/wontfix must record `why`, `alternatives considered`, and `revisit trigger`.
5. Any chronic reopener (`reopen_count >= 2`) is blocking in the next sweep.
6. Sweep is not “done” unless both improve: scores and root-cause closure.

## Public API / Interface / Type Changes
1. One-shot command path migration in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands` with no compatibility shims.
2. Update dynamic command imports in `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py` (`_COMMAND_MAP`) to new module paths in the same migration commit.
3. Standardize cross-language interfaces in:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/deps.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/deps.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/csharp/detectors/deps.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/move.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/move.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/csharp/move.py`
4. Replace optional-parameter sprawl with typed options objects in:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/runtime.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/output/visualize.py`

## Execution Plan (Large Parallel Sweeps)

### Sweep 1: T4 + Chronic + Housing
1. Clear T4 props passthrough findings in:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests/test_gods.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests/test_issues.py`
2. Decompose T4 structural hotspot:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/dict_keys_visitor.py`
3. Re-house flat command directory (`flat_dirs::desloppify/commands`) by moving command modules into grouped subpackages under `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands`.
4. Re-house flat test directory (`flat_dirs::tests`) by moving tests into domain subpackages under `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests`.
5. Eliminate chronic deferred import in:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/scan_reporting_summary.py`

### Sweep 2: Low-Level Elegance / Smells Burn-Down
1. Remove `silent_except` and `swallowed_error` clusters (production/script first).
2. Remove `noop_function` cluster by inlining trivial wrappers or giving real behavior.
3. Resolve `annotation_quality` cluster with concrete type annotations.
4. Resolve `deferred_import` cluster by actual dependency untangling, not suppression.
5. Resolve residual smell families: `hardcoded_path_sep`, `magic_number`, `type_ignore`, `duplicate_constant`, `vestigial_parameter`, `workaround_tag`, `broad_except`.

### Sweep 3: Mid-Level Elegance / Seam Coherence
1. Close all signature variance findings (`build_dep_graph`, `find_replacements`, `find_self_replacements`, `filter_directory_self_changes`) across Python/TypeScript/C# language modules.
2. Close single-use hotspot:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/query.py`
3. Close direct test coverage gaps in:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/dev_scaffold_templates.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/fallbacks.py`

   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/context_models.py`

### Sweep 4: High-Level Elegance / Structural Decomposition
1. Decompose highest-impact production structural files first:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/narrative/actions.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/remediation.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/issues.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/import_findings.py`

2. Decompose structural hotspots in language modules:
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/react.py`
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/typescript/detectors/smells.py`

   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/csharp/detectors/deps.py`
3. Split giant test files into scenario-focused modules while fixing inline-class/mutable-default/unsafe-write smell patterns.

### Sweep 5: Subjective Elegance Lift Loop (Every Sweep)
1. Run after code changes:
   - `desloppify review --prepare --holistic --refresh --dimensions high_level_elegance,mid_level_elegance,low_level_elegance`
   - `desloppify review --prepare --refresh --max-files 50 --dimensions high_level_elegance,mid_level_elegance,low_level_elegance`
2. Import assessments/findings, then rescan immediately.
3. If any elegance dimension remains `<95`, the next sweep backlog must include explicit corrective code actions tied to that dimension.

## Test Cases and Validation
1. Per-commit targeted tests for changed subsystems, plus command dispatch coverage from `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests/test_cli.py` and `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests/test_cmd_*.py`.
2. Per-sweep full gate: `pytest -q`.
3. Per-sweep scan gate: `desloppify scan`.
4. Per-sweep prioritization clarity gate: `desloppify next --count 20`.
5. Per-sweep reopener gate: `desloppify show --chronic`.
6. Final gate:
   - strict `>=95.0`
   - High/Mid/Low Elegance each `>=95.0`
   - T4 objective findings `=0`
   - chronic reopeners `=0`
   - all detector buckets improved versus February 17, 2026 baseline.

## Assumptions and Defaults
1. Active scoring state is `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/.desloppify/state-python.json`.
2. One-shot path migration is accepted (no temporary shims).
3. Large sweeps are preferred over micro-batches.
4. Tactical suppressions are allowed across zones only when justified and logged.
5. Subjective refresh happens every sweep but is always code-delta-gated by rationale.
